1	bzip2/libbzip2: internal error number %d.
1	This is a bug in bzip2/libbzip2, %s.
1	Please report it to me at: jseward@bzip.org.  If this happened
1	when you were using some program which uses libbzip2 as a
1	component, you should also report this bug to the author(s)
1	of that program.  Please make an effort to report this bug;
1	timely and accurate bug reports eventually lead to higher
1	quality software.  Thanks.  Julian Seward, 10 December 2007.
1	*** A special note about internal error number 1007 ***
1	Experience suggests that a common cause of i.e. 1007
1	is unreliable memory or other hardware.  The 1007 assertion
1	just happens to cross-check the results of huge numbers of
1	memory reads/writes, and so acts (unintendedly) as a stress
1	test of your memory system.
1	I suggest the following: try compressing the file again,
1	possibly monitoring progress in detail with the -vv flag.
1	* If the error cannot be reproduced, and/or happens at different
1	  points in compression, you may have a flaky memory system.
1	  Try a memory-test program.  I have used Memtest86
1	  (www.memtest86.com).  At the time of writing it is free (GPLd).
1	  Memtest86 tests memory much more thorougly than your BIOSs
1	  power-on test, and may find failures that the BIOS doesn't.
1	* If the error can be repeatably reproduced, this is a bug in
1	  bzip2, and I would very much like to hear about it.  Please
1	  let me know, and, ideally, save a copy of the file causing the
1	  problem -- without which I will be unable to investigate it.
